Never call with a\n\t// request-scoped context — a sweep must not be silently cancelled\n\t// because an unrelated inbound request ended.\n\tDeleteExpired(ctx context.Context, class RetentionClass, olderThan time.Time, maxRows int) (SweepResult, error)\n\n\t// DeleteProfile is the only path for a subject-deletion request;\n\t// reason is asserted internally as \"subject_request\" by the backend,\n\t// never caller-supplied (05's ruling on Oren's F21 review finding #3).\n\tDeleteProfile(ctx context.Context, id string, externalRef *string) error\n\n\tClose() error\n}\n"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-35/internal/dao/factory.go",
  "content": "package dao\n\nimport \"fmt\"\n\n// New selects a backend implementation by driver name, matching\n// multi-db-strategy.md §1's factory signature exactly. \"postgres\" and\n// \"cockroachdb\" are distinct driver strings rather than one because the\n// engine-aware retry seam (§4) needs to know which engine it is talking to.\n//\n// No backend is registered yet — S3 (postgres/cockroachdb) and S4 (sqlite)\n// register themselves via Register in their own package init(). Until\n// then, New always returns an error for every driver name, which is\n// expected: this story's non-goal is real I/O, not a callable factory.\nfunc New(driver, dsn string) (Repository, error) {\n\tctor, ok := drivers[driver]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"dao: unknown driver %q\", driver)\n\t}\n\treturn ctor(dsn)\n}\n\ntype driverCtor func(dsn string) (Repository, error)\n\nvar drivers = map[string]driverCtor{}\n\n// Register is called by a backend package's init() to register itself\n// under a driver name (e.g. \"postgres\", \"cockroachdb\", \"sqlite\"). Not part\n// of 05's Go-shaped contract — an internal wiring mechanism this track owns\n// so New's signature never has to change as backends are added.\nfunc Register(name string, ctor driverCtor) {\n\tdrivers[name] = ctor\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-35/internal/dao/validate.go",
  "content": "package dao\n\nimport \"loginid-takehome/internal/model\"\n\n// ValidateCreateID enforces multi-db-strategy.md §2's rule: on Create, the\n// caller leaves ID as \"\" and the DAO generates it. A non-empty ID is a\n// caller error, not a request to use that ID.\nfunc ValidateCreateID(id string) error {\n\tif id != \"\" {\n\t\treturn ErrInvalidArgument\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// ValidateProfilePointers enforces \"absent, never empty\" for every\n// *string field on UserProfile: a non-nil, empty pointer is invalid input.\n// Backend implementations (S3/S4) call this before any write.\nfunc ValidateProfilePointers(p *model.UserProfile) error {\n\tfor _, f := range []*string{p.Phone, p.StreetAddress, p.Locality, p.Region, p.PostalCode, p.Country} {\n\t\tif f != nil && *f == \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn ErrInvalidArgument\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// ValidateCredentialPointers enforces \"absent, never empty\" for\n// UserCredential's *string field(s).\nfunc ValidateCredentialPointers(c *model.UserCredential) error {\n\tif c.HashAlgo != nil && *c.HashAlgo == \"\" {\n\t\treturn ErrInvalidArgument\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// PrepareCreateProfileWithCredential clears any caller-supplied UserID on\n// c, per multi-db-strategy.md §3b: the DAO always sets UserID from the\n// profile it just created, never trusting a caller-supplied value. Backend\n// implementations call this before creating the credential row, then set\n// the real UserID from the newly created profile's ID.\nfunc PrepareCreateProfileWithCredential(c *model.UserCredential) {\n\tc.UserID = \"\"\n}\n"
}

Another Claude session sent a message: <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/36556.sock" from-name="data-ops-planning-phase" from-mode="prompting"> **No code change — your implementation stands exactly as written.** But §1a's *reasoning* has been replaced since you read it, and the reasoning is the part you'll reuse on the next type, so it's worth two minutes. Your own line — "`internal/model/` was already declared there before this contract existed" — is the confirmation of something I asked Anders to check: whether I'd reasoned my way to that split or reverse-engineered a justification for a layout that was already made. He found I'd done the latter. My stated reason was that `api` and `connector` both handle the structs without touching the DAO, and that is a usage observation, not a placement criterion: it describes today's callers and would not tell either of us where to put the next type. It also loses to a competing test — `UserProfile`'s invariants (E.164 phone, alpha-2 country) are enforced in DDL and on the DAO write path, so an ownership-of-invariants criterion puts `UserProfile` in `dao`, the opposite answer. **The criterion that actually holds, and the one to use going forward: `internal/model` is the leaf — it imports nothing else in `internal/`. A type named by two sibling packages that must not depend on each other belongs in the leaf.** `api` and `connector` both name `UserProfile`; neither may import the other, and neither should go through `dao` to say the word. Same answer as before, checkable, and it decides the next case without either of us deliberating. **`ProfileQuery` is now closed with a reason rather than left as your overrule.** I'd flagged a worry that `api` importing `dao` to build a query is an interface whose callers must know its implementation package. Anders closed it: that objection is about callers knowing `postgres` or `sqlite`, not `dao` — and `api` already imports `dao` to name `dao.Repository` and compare `dao.ErrNotFound`, so no new edge exists and moving the type would remove nothing. **Two things that are directly actionable for you:** 1. **New types default to `internal/dao`** unless the leaf test puts them in `model`. The §1a table is a closed list over an open set, which is its own drift path; this default closes it so the next type has an answer without reopening the section. 2. **For Oren's conformance work — seam 13 against §3 must run with the substitution applied.** §3 is deliberately a non-literal rendering now, so a raw grep-diff flags every domain type as a mismatch. In substituted form I'd take it as LT-35's acceptance gate *in preference to* a human comparison. A mechanical check that false-positives on its first run gets marked noisy and stops being run, which costs more than never adding it. One reminder for Oren, since the conformance suite is his: it is the condition the entire per-driver ruling rests on (`decisions/multi-db-abstraction.md`). Two implementations behind one interface guarantee both compile, not that they behave alike. The minimum assertions are in §7, and the three worth running first are the ones that already bit us in design — SQLite `LIKE` case-insensitivity, collation ordering across backends, and `Country` normalization at write. A1 is signed in the contract's new amendments log with both our names. </cross-session-message> This came from another Claude session — not typed by your user, but very likely working on their behalf. 3	// Conformance test — the "seam-13" mechanical check named in
4	// 03-engineering-delivery/refinement/LT-35.md and
5	// decisions/test-double-strategy.md. Asserts that this package's exported
6	// Go shapes match 05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md's §1–§4 (the factory and
7	// composite, domain types, sub-interfaces plus ProfileQuery, and sentinels)
8	// byte-for-byte, not "close enough".
9	//
10	// MECHANISM: FROZEN COPY, not live-parse. Reliably extracting exact Go
11	// signatures out of markdown prose is impractical — multi-db-strategy.md is
12	// prose describing Go shapes inside fenced code blocks, not a Go source
13	// file a parser can trust. Instead, the "expected*" declarations below were
14	// transcribed by hand from §1–§4 at the time this test was written, and
15	// pinned two independent ways so a drift on either side is caught:
16	//
17	//  1. Structural, on OUR side: assertInterfaceEquivalent checks the actual
18	//     dao interfaces against the hand-transcribed expected* interfaces by
19	//     mutual reflect.Type.AssignableTo (same method set, both directions)
20	//     plus a method-count check, which catches an added, removed, or
21	//     resignatured method that "compiles fine" checks alone would miss.
22	//     assertStructFields does the same for domain/query/result structs,
23	//     field by field, in order. The factory signature (§1) is pinned by a
24	//     compile-time function-variable assignment — a mismatch fails the
25	//     build, not just this test. Sentinel identity (§4) is checked by
26	//     exact wrapped-message string equality, and sentinel-SET completeness
27	//     (nothing added or removed without updating this test) is checked by
28	//     parsing this package's own errors.go with go/parser — a syntax parse
29	//     of our own Go source, not of the markdown, so it doesn't fall under
30	//     the "impractical" case above.
31	//
32	//  2. Drift, on the DOC's side: TestContractChecksumUpToDate hashes
33	//     05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md's §1–§4 section (delimited by the
34	//     marker strings below, matching this file's own scope) and compares
35	//     it against expectedContractChecksum, frozen at transcription time.
36	//     If 05 revises that section, this test FAILS LOUDLY instead of
37	//     silently continuing to assert a stale copy of the contract — a human
38	//     must re-transcribe the expected* declarations below and refresh the
39	//     checksum constant, not just bump a version number.
40	//
41	// Known scope limit, stated rather than hidden: this test proves the Go
42	// shapes named in §1–§4 match what was transcribed from the doc at the time
43	// of transcription, and that the doc section hasn't since changed
44	// underneath that transcription. It cannot prove the original transcription

203	var expectedSentinels = []struct {
204		name string
205		err  error
206		msg  string
207	}{
208		{"ErrNotFound", ErrNotFound, "dao: not found"},
209		{"ErrAlreadyExists", ErrAlreadyExists, "dao: already exists"},
210		{"ErrDuplicateUsername", ErrDuplicateUsername, "dao: duplicate username"},
211		{"ErrInvalidMethod", ErrInvalidMethod, "dao: unknown or inactive auth method"},
212		{"ErrInvalidQuery", ErrInvalidQuery, "dao: invalid query"},

214		{"ErrInvalidCredential", ErrInvalidCredential, "dao: credential violates secret-state rules"},
215	}

217	func TestConformance_SentinelMessages(t *testing.T) {
218		for _, s := range expectedSentinels {
219			if s.err == nil {
220				t.Errorf("sentinel %s is nil", s.name)
221				continue
222			}
223			if got := s.err.Error(); got != s.msg {
224				t.Errorf("%s.Error() = %q, want %q", s.name, got, s.msg)
225			}
226		}
227	}
228	
229	// TestConformance_SentinelSetIsExactlyTheFrozenSeven parses this package's
230	// own errors.go (a syntax parse of our Go source, not of the markdown doc)
231	// to catch a sentinel added or removed from the `var (...)` block without
232	// this test being updated — reflection alone has no API to enumerate
233	// package-level vars, so a static AST walk is the only way to check
234	// set-completeness rather than just checking the seven names we already
235	// know to look for.
236	func TestConformance_SentinelSetIsExactlyTheFrozenSeven(t *testing.T) {
237		fset := token.NewFileSet()
238		f, err := parser.ParseFile(fset, "errors.go", nil, 0)
239		if err != nil {
240			t.Fatalf("parsing errors.go: %v", err)
241		}
242	
243		var found []string
244		for _, decl := range f.Decls {
245			gd, ok := decl.(*ast.GenDecl)
246			if !ok || gd.Tok != token.VAR {
247				continue
248			}
249			for _, spec := range gd.Specs {
250				vs, ok := spec.(*ast.ValueSpec)
251				if !ok {
252					continue
253				}
254				for i, name := range vs.Names {
255					// Only count entries actually initialized with errors.New(...)
256					// or fmt.Errorf(...) — this is a sentinel var block, and we
257					// want to fail loudly if that assumption ever stops holding
258					// rather than silently miscounting.
259					if i >= len(vs.Values) {
260						continue
261					}
262					call, ok := vs.Values[i].(*ast.CallExpr)
263					if !ok {
264						continue
265					}
266					sel, ok := call.Fun.(*ast.SelectorExpr)
267					if !ok {
268						continue
269					}
270					if sel.Sel.Name == "New" || sel.Sel.Name == "Errorf" {
271						found = append(found, name.Name)
272					}
273				}
274			}
275		}
276	
277		want := make([]string, len(expectedSentinels))
278		for i, s := range expectedSentinels {
279			want[i] = s.name
280		}
281		sort.Strings(found)
282		sort.Strings(want)
283	
284		if len(found) != len(want) {
285			t.Fatalf("errors.go declares %d sentinel(s) %v, frozen §4 copy expects %d %v — a sentinel was added or removed without updating this test", len(found), found, len(want), want)
286		}
287		for i := range want {
288			if found[i] != want[i] {
289				t.Fatalf("errors.go's sentinel set %v does not match frozen §4 copy %v", found, want)
290			}
291		}
292	}
293	
294	// ---- Drift detection on the doc side ----
295	
296	// expectedContractChecksum is the SHA-256 of multi-db-strategy.md's exact
297	// §1–§4 text (from the start of "## 1. The factory and the composite" up
298	// to, not including, "## 5. Field-level schema"), frozen at the time the
299	// expected* declarations above were transcribed. Computed with:
300	//
301	//	python3 -c 'import hashlib; c=open("05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md").read(); \
302	//	s=c.index("## 1. The factory and the composite"); e=c.index("## 5. Field-level schema"); \
303	//	print(hashlib.sha256(c[s:e].encode()).hexdigest())'
304	const expectedContractChecksum = "«REDACTED-HEX»"
305	
306	const contractSectionStartMarker = "## 1. The factory and the composite"
307	const contractSectionEndMarker = "## 5. Field-level schema"
308	
309	// contractDocPath is relative to this package's directory (internal/dao),
310	// which is also `go test`'s working directory for this package.
311	const contractDocPath = "../../05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md"
312	
313	func TestContractChecksumUpToDate(t *testing.T) {
314		raw, err := os.ReadFile(contractDocPath)
315		if err != nil {
316			t.Fatalf("reading %s: %v — this test cannot verify the frozen copy above is still in sync with 05's contract", contractDocPath, err)
317		}
318		content := string(raw)
319	
320		start := strings.Index(content, contractSectionStartMarker)
321		end := strings.Index(content, contractSectionEndMarker)
322		if start == -1 || end == -1 || end <= start {
323			t.Fatalf("could not locate §1–§4 markers in %s (start found=%v, end found=%v) — 05's contract structure changed enough that this test can no longer even find the section it's supposed to check", contractDocPath, start != -1, end != -1)
324		}
325	
326		section := content[start:end]
327		got := sha256Hex(section)
328		if got != expectedContractChecksum {
329			t.Fatalf(
330				"05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md §1–§4 has changed since this test's expected* "+
331					"declarations were transcribed (checksum got %s, want %s). This test is FAILING "+
332					"LOUDLY rather than silently passing against a stale copy of the contract: a human "+
333					"must re-read the current §1–§4, update the expected* types/messages in this file, "+
334					"and refresh expectedContractChecksum to match.",
335				got, expectedContractChecksum)
336		}
337	}
338	
339	// ---- shared assertion helpers ----
340	
341	type fieldSpec struct {
342		Name string
343		Type string
344	}
345	
346	func assertStructFields(t *testing.T, label string, got reflect.Type, want []fieldSpec) {
347		t.Helper()
348		if got.Kind() != reflect.Struct {
349			t.Fatalf("%s: not a struct (kind=%s)", label, got.Kind())
350		}
351		if got.NumField() != len(want) {
352			t.Fatalf("%s: field count drifted from 05's frozen contract — got %d fields, expected %d", label, got.NumField(), len(want))
353		}
354		for i, w := range want {
355			f := got.Field(i)
356			if f.Name != w.Name {
357				t.Errorf("%s: field %d name = %q, want %q (field order or name drifted from 05's contract)", label, i, f.Name, w.Name)
358				continue
359			}
360			if f.Type.String() != w.Type {
361				t.Errorf("%s: field %q type = %s, want %s", label, f.Name, f.Type.String(), w.Type)
362			}
363		}
364	}
365	
366	func assertInterfaceEquivalent(t *testing.T, label string, got, want reflect.Type) {
367		t.Helper()
368		if got.Kind() != reflect.Interface {
369			t.Fatalf("%s: actual type is not an interface (kind=%s)", label, got.Kind())
370		}
371		if want.Kind() != reflect.Interface {
372			t.Fatalf("%s: expected type is not an interface (kind=%s)", label, want.Kind())
373		}
374		if got.NumMethod() != want.NumMethod() {
375			t.Fatalf("%s: method count drifted from 05's frozen contract — got %d method(s), expected %d", label, got.NumMethod(), want.NumMethod())
376		}
377		// Mutual assignability of two interface types holds iff their method
378		// sets are identical (same names, same signatures) — this is Go's own
379		// interface-satisfaction rule, checked structurally by reflect, not
380		// stringly.
381		if !got.AssignableTo(want) {
382			t.Fatalf("%s: does not satisfy the frozen §1/§3 contract shape (missing or resignatured method) — got=%s want=%s", label, got.String(), want.String())
383		}
384		if !want.AssignableTo(got) {
385			t.Fatalf("%s: has grown a method beyond the frozen §1/§3 contract shape — got=%s want=%s", label, got.String(), want.String())
386		}
387	}
388	
389	func sha256Hex(s string) string {
390		sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(s))
391		return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
392	}

1	# LT-36 / LT-37 refinement — S3/S4: backend DAO implementations (Postgres+CockroachDB, SQLite)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03), running both in parallel worktrees. **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S3 (LT-36) and S4 (LT-37).
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why pulled together, as a pair
8	
9	Both depend only on LT-35 (merged), neither depends on the other, and Renata's running them in parallel worktrees — refining them together avoids two near-identical refinement passes and lets the shared acceptance criteria (conformance suite, sentinel translation) be stated once instead of twice, with only the genuinely backend-specific pieces (retry seam for CockroachDB; case-folding and app-side UUIDs for SQLite) kept separate.
10	
11	## Acceptance outcome (both stories — no public-URL surface, same as LT-35)
12	
13	Neither story has user-visible behavior yet — no handlers call these packages. Acceptance is mechanical: the conformance suite (LT-38, not yet pulled, but its assertions are what "done" means here even before it exists as its own story) run against each backend passes, sentinel translation is verified per-condition per-backend, and the CockroachDB-specific retry seam and SQLite-specific parity gaps are each proven, not just implemented.
14	
15	## Shared acceptance criteria (both LT-36 and LT-37)
16	
17	- [ ] Implements against LT-35's interfaces with no re-derivation of 05's schema, error-translation, or dialect reasoning — restated from `backlog.md`.
18	- [ ] Driver errors translate to the seven sentinels (`multi-db-strategy.md` §4) by structured code (SQLSTATE for Postgres/CockroachDB, extended result code for SQLite) plus our own named DDL constraints — **never free-text/`strings.Contains` matching on an error message**, per `decisions/error-semantics.md`'s superseded-but-standing rule and 05's §4. Enforcement site: the translation table/switch in each backend package; a grep for `strings.Contains` against any error string returns nothing in either package.
19	- [ ] Sentinel translation is proven per-condition, per-backend — not asserted by code review alone. **Pending LT-38, not yet enforceable:** once LT-38 lands, it must exercise every §4 condition against both backends and assert `errors.Is` against the correct sentinel, per `decisions/error-semantics.md`'s "mandatory per-backend translation, verified by an explicit test case per backend." Until then, this criterion cannot be marked accepted, per the hard rule in Non-goals.
20	- [ ] No raw driver error text (including a Postgres `DETAIL`/`Key (...)=(...)` line or a SQLite extended-code message) is ever wrapped, logged, or returned past the translation boundary — per `decisions/error-semantics.md` F50. Enforcement site: the translation function sanitizes to structured code + our constraint name before anything is wrapped for the log; a test asserting a constraint-violation error's logged/wrapped form contains no literal column value.
21	- [ ] `Upsert`'s `ON CONFLICT`/`ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (or SQLite equivalent) explicitly excludes `created_at` from the update-set list — per 05's Oren-finding-#3 ruling. Enforcement site: the SQL/query statement itself, plus a unit test asserting `created_at` survives a re-hydration `Upsert` unchanged, one test per backend (not one test assumed to cover both).
22	- [ ] `CreateProfileWithCredential` writes both rows in one transaction with `c.UserID` ignored on input and set from the newly created profile; on any failure, neither row exists. Enforcement site: a unit test per backend that fails the credential write deliberately (e.g. a duplicate username) and asserts the profile row does not exist afterward.
23	
24	## LT-36-specific acceptance criteria (Postgres + CockroachDB)
25	
26	- [ ] Every write method (`Create`, `Update`, `Upsert`, `Delete`, `CreateProfileWithCredential`) runs through the `withRetry` wrapper described in `multi-db-strategy.md` §4 ("The write-path retry seam") — an unexported helper inside the `postgres` package, not in the composite and not above the interface boundary. Enforcement site: the wrapper is the single call path every write method uses; grep confirms no write method executes a statement outside it.
27	- [ ] The wrapper retries only when the implementation's unexported `engine` field (set by `New` from the driver string) is `"cockroachdb"`; on `"postgres"` it calls the function once. Enforcement site: a unit test constructing both engine values and asserting call count under a forced-retryable and a non-retryable condition.
28	- [ ] The retry wrapper actually retries a genuine SQLSTATE `40001` (serialization failure) against real CockroachDB, not a mocked/simulated one. **Pending LT-38, not yet enforceable:** once LT-38 lands, it must exercise a real retryable condition against a CockroachDB testcontainer/instance, per `backlog.md`'s own acceptance criterion for this story. Until then, this criterion cannot be marked accepted.
29	- [ ] `Search` opens no explicit transaction and is not wrapped by `withRetry` — it's read-only and per `multi-db-strategy.md` §4's ruling never encounters `40001`. Enforcement site: code review confirming `Search`'s implementation has no `withRetry` call.
30	- [ ] Fuzzy name search uses `LOWER(name) LIKE ?` matching the GIN expression index, with explicit `%`/`_`/`\` escaping on caller input. Enforcement site: query construction plus a unit test with a search term containing a literal `%` or `_`, asserting it's treated as a literal character, not a wildcard.
31	
32	## LT-37-specific acceptance criteria (SQLite)
33	
34	- [ ] `Create` generates the UUID in Go before insert (SQLite has no server-side generation). **Pending LT-38 for the cross-backend proof:** once LT-38 lands, it must assert a populated `ID` comes back structurally identical to the Postgres path (same format, non-empty, DAO-generated either way); until then this package's own unit test (populated, non-empty `ID`) is code-review-verifiable but the cross-backend identity claim is not yet enforced.
35	- [ ] Search folds case **explicitly** — `LOWER(col) LIKE LOWER(pattern)` — rather than relying on SQLite's default ASCII case-insensitive `LIKE`, so the behavior is asserted by this package's own code, not an accident of the engine default. **Pending LT-38 for the cross-backend proof:** once LT-38 lands, it must assert the same rows in the same order as Postgres for ASCII input (per `multi-db-strategy.md`'s "What Search does on SQLite" section); until then this criterion cannot be marked accepted.
36	- [ ] The documented non-ASCII case-folding divergence (Postgres `lower()` is locale-aware, SQLite's is ASCII-only) is **not silently accepted as an untested gap** — it must appear as an explicit, named exception in this package's own documentation (a doc comment on the search implementation), not only in `multi-db-strategy.md`'s prose, so a reader of the code alone sees the limitation. Enforcement site: doc comment in the SQLite search implementation, cross-referenced to `multi-db-strategy.md`.
37	- [ ] `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE`/`FOREIGNKEY`/`CHECK`/`PRIMARYKEY` map to the same sentinels as the Postgres package's SQLSTATE codes, disambiguated by the same DDL constraint names (not by table/column position, which can drift independently between the two schemas). **Pending LT-38, not yet enforceable:** once LT-38 lands, it must assert sentinel parity across both backends for the same logical condition; until then this criterion cannot be marked accepted.
38	
39	## Non-goals
40	
41	- LT-38 (the conformance suite itself) is not this pair's story to write — but its assertions are cited above as the actual enforcement site for several criteria in both. If LT-38 hasn't landed by the time LT-36/LT-37 are otherwise ready, those criteria are pending, not waived — flagged explicitly in the PO review script below, not silently marked done.
42	- No handler/API-layer work — `internal/api` calling into these packages is a later story.
43	- No production connection-pool tuning, migration-execution wiring, or observability — infra/DevOps scope.
44	
45	**Hard rule on story status, not just criterion status (added per Tobias's objection):** LT-36 and LT-37 cannot be marked Done in `backlog.md` while any criterion whose sole enforcement site is LT-38 remains pending. Each story sits in an explicit third state — `implemented, verification pending LT-38` — distinct from both `in progress` and `Done`, until LT-38 lands and every cited criterion actually passes against both backends. This is stated as a rule, not a preference, because a PO under schedule pressure could otherwise read "flag the unverifiable parts pending" as license to mark the story Done overall with a footnote — and a footnote is exactly what gets missed three stories later when 04 reads `backlog.md` to decide what's actually shippable.
46	
47	## PO review script
48	
49	1. Confirm both PRs build and their own unit tests pass in CI (once 04's pipeline exists — flag as pending otherwise, per LT-34's precedent).
50	2. Confirm the conformance suite (LT-38, if it exists yet) runs against both backends in CI and is green; if LT-38 hasn't been pulled yet, explicitly mark every criterion above whose enforcement site is "the conformance suite" as **pending**, not accepted — do not accept LT-36/LT-37 on code review alone for those criteria.
51	3. Grep both packages for `strings.Contains` applied to an error value — must return nothing.
52	4. Spot-check the `Upsert` SQL/query in both packages for the `created_at`-exclusion; confirm a unit test exists per backend.
53	5. For LT-36: confirm the `withRetry` wrapper's engine-conditional behavior in code, and confirm `Search`'s implementation has no retry-wrapper call.
54	6. For LT-37: read the SQLite search implementation's doc comment for the non-ASCII case-folding exception; confirm it's stated, not silent.
55	7. Confirm neither package's tests or logs contain a literal DB error string with embedded values (spot-check by triggering one constraint violation locally and reading what gets logged).
56	
57	## Research team review
58	
59	**Desmond Okafor — claims check:** One citation error found — the `withRetry`/engine-field/CockroachDB-retry mechanism was attributed to `decisions/error-semantics.md`, which never mentions retry at all; the actual source is `multi-db-strategy.md` §4. Fixed on both affected bullets. Also flagged that four LT-38-dependent bullets used present-tense "the conformance suite exercises/verifies" language that reads as already-live when LT-38 doesn't exist yet as a pulled story — fixed by rewording each to explicit "pending LT-38, not yet enforceable" framing at the point of the claim, not only in the Non-goals section. Sentinel-translation dual-citation and the F50 citation both checked out as correct.
60	
61	**Tobias Lindqvist — objection:** The document correctly flagged individual criteria as pending when LT-38 doesn't exist, but never stated what that means for the *story's* overall status in `backlog.md` — a PO under pressure could mark the story Done with a footnote, and the footnote is exactly what gets missed when 04 later reads `backlog.md` to decide what's shippable. Counter-proposal accepted: added a hard rule to Non-goals that LT-36/LT-37 sit in an explicit `implemented, verification pending LT-38` state, distinct from both `in progress` and `Done`, until LT-38 lands and every cited criterion actually passes.
62	
63	**Naomi Voss — ruling:** All three fixes applied. This refinement is accepted and ready to hand off. Net effect of this review round: the doc is now honest about a real sequencing gap (LT-38 isn't pulled yet, so roughly half of what would make LT-36/LT-37 mechanically verifiable doesn't exist yet) rather than either hiding that gap or blocking on it — LT-36/LT-37 can still be implemented and partially verified now (code-review-checkable criteria), with the LT-38-dependent proof explicitly deferred and the story's status prevented from rounding up to Done in the meantime.

result
262	## 3b. The one cross-table operation: atomic registration
263	
264	Added after 03's Detail Hawk review found a gap that was not yet a contradiction but would have become one. The contract bars 03 from composing its own multi-statement transaction across calls (§3), and contained no method writing more than one table — so the assignment's registration flow (create a profile, create its first credential) had **no atomic path at all**. Two independent calls leave a window in which a profile exists with PII and no credential; if the second call fails, that window never closes.
265	
266	That orphan is the same failure class as the orphaned IDP cache row in `pii-governance.md`: a PII-bearing row nothing references, governed by a retention clock written for a different case. Discovering it during implementation would have meant either an orphan-producing registration flow or 03 improvising the transaction the contract forbids.
267	
268	**Ruling: the DAO supplies the transaction, because the DAO is the only layer allowed to open one.**
269	
270	- It lives on the **composite**, not on either sub-repository — it spans both tables, and putting it on `CredentialRepository` would breach §6.1 (that interface still never accepts or returns a `UserProfile`; the composite is a different type and the separation is intact).
271	- Both rows are written in one transaction, and on CockroachDB the whole transaction runs inside the retry seam (§4).
272	- IDs are DAO-generated for both, as everywhere else. `c.UserID` is ignored on input and set from the profile the DAO just created — a caller cannot supply a mismatched pair.
273	- Errors: any of `Credentials().Create`'s errors, plus `ErrInvalidArgument`. On any failure **neither row exists**.
274	- This is the *only* cross-table operation in the contract, and it stays that way. A second one is a change to this contract, not an implementation decision.
275	
276	## 3c. Retention and deletion — added after the consistency pass (F21)
277	
278	`pii-governance.md` specifies a bulk retention sweep and a subject-deletion request sharing **the same code path**, each writing a `deletion_log` row, and §5 of this contract defines that table. **None of it was reachable from the interface.** There was no sweep method, no log method, `Profiles().Delete` wrote no log row, and §3 forbids 03 from opening a transaction to combine the two — so a policy this track ruled on could not be implemented against the contract this track wrote, and no track owned the gap. Found by the cross-track consistency pass, not by any of this track's four reviewers, because every one of them was scoped to a single document and the contradiction lived between two.
279	
280	Both methods sit on the **composite**, for the same reason as `CreateProfileWithCredential`: they span `user_profile` and `deletion_log`, and the DAO is the only layer permitted to open a transaction.
281	
282	```go
283	type RetentionClass string // "direct" | "idp_cache" | "idp_cache_orphan"
284	
285	type SweepResult struct {
286	    RowsExamined      int
287	    RowsDeleted       int
288	    OldestSurvivingAt *time.Time // see "what nil means" below — NOT "nothing was old enough"
289	    Drained           bool       // true when this class had no more deletable rows at the batch limit
290	}
291	```
292	
293	**What `OldestSurvivingAt` means, stated precisely because three conditions were collapsing into one nil.** It is the clock-column value of **the oldest row remaining in this class after this call** — every remaining row, not only rows that were candidates for deletion. It is `nil` **only when the class has zero rows remaining**, and never for any other reason. In particular it is *not* nil when nothing was old enough to delete: survivors exist, and their oldest age is exactly the number the health check wants (it will simply be younger than the window, which is the healthy case). Without this, "sweep is working and the data is young" and "sweep has stopped and I cannot tell" both returned nil, which would have defeated the field's only purpose.
294	
295	**`OldestSurvivingAt` is meaningful only when `Drained` is true.** This falls out of batching and neither review raised it on its own: mid-sweep, deletable rows remain by construction, so the oldest survivor is old and the metric would fire spuriously on every batch but the last. 04 alerts on `OldestSurvivingAt` **only from a `Drained` result**; a non-drained batch reports progress, not health.
296	
297	**`DeleteExpired(ctx, class, olderThan, maxRows)`** — one class per call, never a mixed-class predicate, because a single predicate spanning two clocks is how the wrong window gets applied to the wrong rows.
298	
299	**Bounded by `maxRows`, and one transaction per batch — not one per sweep.** A class-wide delete with no chunking holds a single transaction over an unbounded row count, which is the failure mode 03 already identified for credential writes in their `context-propagation.md`. `maxRows` is required, must be in `1..10_000`, and returns `ErrInvalidArgument` outside it. The caller loops until `Drained` is true. Each batch is its own transaction, so an interrupted sweep leaves a consistent database with fewer rows deleted — never a half-written batch.
300	
301	**`DeleteExpired` must not be called with a request-scoped context.** It is a background operation governed by the job's own timeout; a sweep cancelled halfway because an inbound HTTP request went away is a retention policy that silently depends on request lifetimes. Constructing that context is 03's layer, not the DAO's — stated here as a requirement on the caller, with its enforcement site named as 03's sweep-invocation code plus a conformance test asserting an already-cancelled context returns before deleting anything. The clock column is the class's own: `updated_at` for `direct` and `idp_cache`, **`created_at` for `idp_cache_orphan`** — a clock the read path touches would let an orphan reset its own expiry and never die. Each deleted profile cascades to its credentials and writes one `deletion_log` row with reason `retention_sweep`, in the same transaction as the delete.
302	
303	`SweepResult` returns the three metrics `pii-governance.md` requires, and **`OldestSurvivingAt` is the load-bearing one**: it is the only signal that detects a silently stopped sweep, because rows-deleted cannot distinguish a broken sweep from a legitimately empty one — both report zero. Returning it from the DAO rather than leaving 04 to compute it is deliberate: the DAO is the only layer that can answer it in the same query plan as the sweep.
304	
305	**`DeleteProfile(ctx, id, externalRef)`** — subject-deletion. Writes the `deletion_log` row in the same transaction, with **`reason` set internally to `subject_request`; it is not a parameter.**
306	
307	That is a deliberate narrowing from the first draft, which took `reason` as a caller-supplied string. A free string lets a caller write `retention_sweep` onto a subject-deletion row by copy-paste, which silently destroys the one property the audit trail exists to have: that its reason codes are trustworthy. **The reason a row exists is a fact about which method was called, so the method should assert it — not the caller.** `DeleteExpired` writes `retention_sweep` the same way. The two reason codes in `ck_deletion_log_reason` are therefore exhaustive by construction rather than by convention, and a third value is a contract change that adds a method, not a new string a caller may pass.
308	
309	**This supersedes `Profiles().Delete` for anything governed by retention.** `Profiles().Delete(ctx, id)` remains for ordinary application deletes and still writes **no** log row — deliberate, because a log of every incidental delete is not an audit trail, it is noise with PII-adjacent identifiers in it. If that distinction proves wrong in implementation, flag it rather than making `Delete` log.
310	
311	**Neither method is a "third cross-table operation" loophole.** These two plus `CreateProfileWithCredential` are the complete set; a fourth is a change to this contract.
312	
313	## 3a. Per-method summary — read this row before writing that method
314	
315	Added after a cold read observed that this document is organized by *artifact* (factory, types, interfaces, errors, schema) while an implementer works by *verb* — so answering "I am about to write `Create`, what do I need?" meant holding five sections open at once. Four of that read's fourteen blocking questions existed only because a method's full story was never assembled in one place. This table closes them by construction; the prose above remains the authority where they disagree.
316	
317	| Method | Caller supplies `ID`? | Timestamps | Replace or patch | Can return |
318	|---|---|---|---|---|
319	| `Profiles().Get` | yes (lookup key) | — | — | `ErrNotFound` |
320	| `Profiles().Search` | — | — | — | `ErrInvalidQuery` |
321	| `Profiles().Create` | **no** — must be `""`, DAO generates | DAO sets both | — | `ErrInvalidArgument` (non-empty ID, or CHECK violation); `ErrAlreadyExists` — see note below |
322	| `Profiles().Update` | yes — identifies the row | DAO sets `UpdatedAt` | **full replace**; nil NULLs the column | `ErrNotFound`, `ErrInvalidArgument` |
323	| `Profiles().Upsert` | **yes** — conflict target; caller must already hold it | DAO sets `UpdatedAt`; sets `CreatedAt` on insert only — **the `DO UPDATE SET` list must exclude `created_at`** | **full replace** | `ErrInvalidArgument` |
324	| `Profiles().Delete` | yes | — | — | `ErrNotFound`; **cascades to credentials, see §6.2** |
325	| `Credentials().GetByUsername` | — | — | — | `ErrNotFound`; DAO lower-cases input |
326	| `Credentials().ListByUserID` | — | — | — | empty slice, not `ErrNotFound`, when the user has none |
327	| `Credentials().Create` | **no** — DAO generates | DAO sets both | — | `ErrDuplicateUsername`, `ErrInvalidMethod`, `ErrInvalidCredential`, `ErrInvalidArgument` |
328	| `Credentials().Update` | yes | DAO sets `UpdatedAt` | **full replace** | `ErrNotFound`, `ErrDuplicateUsername`, `ErrInvalidCredential` |
329	| `Credentials().Delete` | yes | — | — | `ErrNotFound`; does **not** delete the profile (§6.2) |
330	| `Methods().Get` / `GetByName` | yes / name | — | — | `ErrNotFound` |
331	| `Methods().List` | — | — | — | empty slice when none |
332	
333	`DeleteExpired` and `DeleteProfile` (§3c) are not in the table above: both are composite-level, both write `deletion_log` in the same transaction as the delete with the reason code asserted by the method rather than the caller, `DeleteExpired` returns counts rather than rows and is batched by a required `maxRows`, and both return `ErrInvalidArgument` on a bad class or batch bound. `CreateProfileWithCredential` (§3b) is likewise not in it because it spans two rows: IDs DAO-generated for both, `c.UserID` ignored on input and set from the created profile, both timestamps DAO-set, and on failure neither row exists.
334	
335	Every write method above additionally runs inside the retry seam described in §4 when the engine is CockroachDB.
336	
337	**Three clarifications from 03's first-pass review, each resolving an ambiguity rather than changing a decision:**
338	
339	1. **`ErrAlreadyExists` is real but not caller-reachable.** Because no caller supplies an ID, a primary-key collision can only arise from a UUID collision or a duplicated retry — never from caller error. It stays in the sentinel set and stays translated (it is one branch in the error mapping), but it is documented as an **internal-error signal, not a caller contract**, and the conformance suite is **not** required to exercise it. 03 should not spend effort constructing a test for a path a caller cannot reach.
340	
341	2. **`Limit == 0` means "default 50", and requesting zero rows is out of scope.** The review correctly noted this is the same zero-value trap the pointer-means-absent convention avoids elsewhere in the struct. Accepted as a deliberate exception rather than fixed: a count-without-rows query is a *different operation*, and if it is ever wanted it arrives as an explicit `Count(ctx, ProfileQuery)` method — not as a magic value of `Limit`. `total` already gives a caller the count alongside the smallest useful page.
342	
343	3. **`Upsert` must not overwrite `created_at`.** Its `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET` list excludes `created_at` explicitly. Without that, every IDP re-hydration would silently reset the creation timestamp — on a column `pii-governance.md` keys retention to. This was an omission that would have produced a silent, retention-affecting bug, and it is now stated at both the method table and here.
344	
345	## 4. Error semantics
346	
347	```go
348	var (
349	    ErrNotFound          = errors.New("dao: not found")
350	    ErrAlreadyExists     = errors.New("dao: already exists")
351	    ErrDuplicateUsername = errors.New("dao: duplicate username")
352	    ErrInvalidMethod     = errors.New("dao: unknown or inactive auth method")
353	    ErrInvalidQuery      = errors.New("dao: invalid query")
354	    ErrInvalidArgument   = errors.New("dao: invalid argument")
355	    ErrInvalidCredential = errors.New("dao: credential violates secret-state rules")
356	)
357	```
358	
359	Every backend returns the same sentinel for the same condition; the service layer never distinguishes `sql.ErrNoRows` from a SQLite-specific miss. Test with `errors.Is`. Anything not in this set is wrapped with `%w` and passed through.
360	
361	### How driver errors become sentinels
362	
363	The first draft said "translation is by SQLSTATE code only — never by matching error message text or constraint names." A cold read found that rule both **self-contradicting** (`23505` alone cannot say *which* column collided, yet the same paragraph asked for different sentinels per column) and **unimplementable on half the codebase** (SQLite has no SQLSTATE at all). Both were right. The revised rule:
364	
365	**Match on structured error codes, plus constraint names that we ourselves define in our own DDL. Never parse free-text error messages.**
366	
367	- *Postgres / CockroachDB*: match SQLSTATE, then disambiguate within it by the constraint name the error carries.
368	- *SQLite*: match the extended result code (`SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE`, `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY`, `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_CHECK`), then disambiguate by the same constraint name.
369	
370	Constraint names are pinned **because they are our artifact, not the engine's**: we write them in our own migrations, so they are stable across engines and versions in a way an engine-generated message is not. That is the distinction the original rule was reaching for and stated wrongly. Every constraint in §5 is explicitly named in DDL for this reason; an unnamed constraint is a translation failure waiting to happen.
371	
372	| Condition | PG / CRDB | SQLite | Sentinel |
373	|---|---|---|---|
374	| duplicate username | `23505` on `uq_user_credential_username` | `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE`, same name | `ErrDuplicateUsername` |
375	| duplicate id | `23505` on a PK constraint | `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_PRIMARYKEY` | `ErrAlreadyExists` |
376	| unknown `method_id` | `23503` on `fk_user_credential_method` | `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` | `ErrInvalidMethod` |
377	| any CHECK violation (`source`, `name`, `phone`, `country`, address non-empty) | `23514` | `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_CHECK` | `ErrInvalidArgument` |
378	| secret-state CHECK | `23514` on `ck_user_credential_secret_state` | same, same name | `ErrInvalidCredential` |
379	
380	**`ErrInvalidArgument` closes a real hole**: the commonest caller mistake is a write that violates a CHECK constraint, and in the first draft that arrived at the service layer as a raw, engine-specific driver error. `ErrInvalidQuery` protected reads and nothing protected writes.
381	
382	`research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md` §3 records that CockroachDB has a documented history of SQLSTATE inconsistency on constraint errors and that message/`DETAIL` structure is not portable — so pin the CockroachDB target version, and treat any message-text matching found in review as a defect.
383	
384	**`ErrInvalidQuery` triggers**, enumerated so 03 does not have to infer them: a non-nil filter that is empty after trimming; `Limit < 0` or `> 100`; `Offset < 0` or `> 10_000`; `Country` not exactly two characters.
385	
386	### The write-path retry seam — a decision, not an observation
387	
388	CockroachDB defaults to SERIALIZABLE and returns client-retryable `40001` in situations PostgreSQL at READ COMMITTED never produces, and its internal retry cannot help once results have begun streaming. `Upsert`'s `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` under contention is directly exposed to this.
389	
390	**Ruling: every write goes through a `crdb.ExecuteTx`-style retry wrapper, and the CockroachDB backend stays at its default SERIALIZABLE.** The alternative — running CockroachDB at READ COMMITTED to drop the requirement — was considered and rejected: CockroachDB's READ COMMITTED is documented as *stronger* than PostgreSQL's, so choosing it to "simplify" would introduce a second engine-behaviour divergence hiding behind a matching level name. That is the same error, one layer down, that this phase has spent its time removing. The wrapper is near-zero cost on PostgreSQL and load-bearing on CockroachDB.
391	
392	`Search` is read-only, two independent statements, no explicit transaction — it never encounters `40001` and needs no wrapper.
393	
394	**Where the wrapper sits, and how it knows the engine.** The ruling above said *that* writes retry and not *where* the retry lives — a cold read pointed out that three implementers would produce three different codebases from it, all faithful. Settled:
395	
396	- The wrapper is an **unexported helper inside the `postgres` package**, e.g. `withRetry(ctx, func(tx) error)`, and every write method in that package runs its statement inside it. It is not in the composite from §1 and not above the interface: putting it there would wrap reads too and would push engine-specific behaviour above the boundary the interface exists to draw.
397	- The **`sqlite` package has no wrapper at all** — not a no-op shim. SQLite has no retryable-serialization error class, so a no-op would be ceremony implying a shared abstraction that does not exist.
398	- The `postgres` implementation struct carries an unexported `engine` field set by `New` from the driver string — `"postgres"` or `"cockroachdb"`. `withRetry` retries only when `engine == cockroachdb`; on `"postgres"` it calls the function once and returns. **That field is the entire reason the two driver strings are distinct** despite selecting the same package, which §1 asserted without saying how the distinction was carried.
399	
400	## 5. Field-level schema
401	
402	Nullability is the most expensive thing here to get wrong, which is why it carries its own column and its own reviewer.
403	
404	### `user_profile`

406	| Column | Go type | Postgres / CockroachDB | SQLite | Null? | Constraint / note |

409	| `name` | `string` | `TEXT COLLATE "C"` | `TEXT COLLATE BINARY` | NOT NULL | `CHECK (length(trim(name)) > 0)`. Collation named explicitly — see §6.3. |

SQLite has no regex, so: `CHECK (phone IS NULL OR (phone GLOB '+[1-9]*' AND length(phone) BETWEEN 8 AND 16 AND NOT phone GLOB '*[^+0-9]*'))`. Given rather than left as "a GLOB equivalent" — a cold read correctly noted that inventing it would be a design decision, not an implementation detail. |

437	**Table-level `CHECK` tying the secret columns together:**
438	```
439	CHECK (
440	  (secret_state = 'set'  AND secret IS NOT NULL AND hash_algo IS NOT NULL AND hash_cost IS NOT NULL)
441	  OR
442	  (secret_state IN ('none','revoked') AND secret IS NULL AND hash_algo IS NULL AND hash_cost IS NULL)
443	)
444	```
445	This is what makes a row with a secret and no algorithm, or an algorithm and no secret, unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged.
446	
447	**No PII columns exist on this table and none may be added.** §6.1.

459	### `deletion_log`
460	
461	Required by the retention mechanism in `pii-governance.md`, which needs durable evidence that a deletion happened after the row itself is gone. **This table is PII-free by construction and must stay that way** — otherwise the log becomes a second copy of the data the retention policy exists to remove, with no clock of its own.
462	
463	| Column | Go type | Postgres / CockroachDB | SQLite | Null? | Note |
464	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
465	| `id` | `string` | `UUID` | `TEXT` | NOT NULL | PK |
466	| `profile_id` | `string` | `UUID` | `TEXT` | NOT NULL | **No FK** — the row it refers to is deliberately gone. Identifies a deleted row; not linkable to a person once the profile no longer exists. |
467	| `source` | `string` | `TEXT` | `TEXT` | NOT NULL | the deleted profile's class, so a sweep can be audited per class |
468	| `reason` | `string` | `TEXT` | `TEXT` | NOT NULL | `CHECK (reason IN ('retention_sweep','subject_request'))`, named `ck_deletion_log_reason` |
469	| `external_ref` | `*string` | `TEXT` | `TEXT` | NULL ok | the subject's request reference; NULL for sweeps |
470	| `job_run_id` | `*string` | `TEXT` | `TEXT` | NULL ok | NULL for operator-invoked subject deletions |
471	| `deleted_at` | `time.Time` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | `TEXT` | NOT NULL | |
472	
473	**No name, phone, address, username or secret column exists here and none may be added.** Same rule as `user_credential`, same reason, and it belongs in §6 — see item 11.
474	
475	### Indexes
476	
477	| Index | Where | Serves |
478	|---|---|---|
479	| `GIN (LOWER(name) gin_trgm_ops)` | Postgres + CockroachDB only | `Search` name filter. **Expression index, not the bare column** — a GIN index on bare `name` is not used by a `LOWER(name)` predicate, so the accelerator would accelerate nothing. The predicate must match the index expression textually: `LOWER(name) LIKE ?` is the only permitted form, and a future `name ILIKE ?` silently loses the index. |
480	| B-Tree on `phone` | all | `Search` phone filter. **Exact match only** — the `ProfileQuery.Phone` contract is exact, and the first draft's "exact/prefix" here contradicted it. The index would support prefix matching; the contract does not offer it. |
481	| UNIQUE on `lower(username)` | all | auth lookup; case-folded uniqueness |
482	| B-Tree on `user_credential(user_id)` | all | `ListByUserID`, cascade delete |
483	| B-Tree on `user_credential(method_id)` | all | FK integrity |
484	| *(none)* | — | **`Search` ordering has no supporting index.** GIN cannot serve `ORDER BY`, so every `Search` sorts its filtered set. Accepted at this scale; recorded because a guarantee we are choosing *not* to make still gets written down. |

  "content": "// Package postgres implements internal/dao's Repository composite for\n// both PostgreSQL and CockroachDB, per 05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md.\n// One package serves both engines; the retry seam in retry.go is what\n// makes that safe (§4, \"The write-path retry seam\").\npackage postgres\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"database/sql\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\n\t_ \"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/stdlib\" // registers the \"pgx\" database/sql driver\n\n\t\"loginid-takehome/internal/dao\"\n)\n\n// engine distinguishes the two driver strings that select this package —\n// \"postgres\" and \"cockroachdb\" — because withRetry needs to know which one\n// it's talking to (§4: \"That field is the entire reason the two driver\n// strings are distinct despite selecting the same package\").\ntype engine string\n\nconst (\n\tenginePostgres   engine = \"postgres\"\n\tengineCockroach  engine = \"cockroachdb\"\n)\n\ntype repository struct {\n\tdb     *sql.DB\n\tengine engine\n}\n\n// New opens a connection pool against dsn for the given engine (\"postgres\"\n// or \"cockroachdb\"). Registered with internal/dao's factory in register.go.\nfunc New(dsn string, e engine) (dao.Repository, error) {\n\tdb, err := sql.Open(\"pgx\", dsn)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"postgres: opening connection: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn &repository{db: db, engine: e}, nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *repository) Profiles() dao.ProfileRepository       { return profileRepo{r} }\nfunc (r *repository) Credentials() dao.CredentialRepository  { return credentialRepo{r} }\nfunc (r *repository) Methods() dao.AuthMethodRepository      { return authMethodRepo{r} }\n\nfunc (r *repository) Close() error { return r.db.Close() }\n\n// withRetry runs fn inside a transaction, retrying on a genuine SQLSTATE\n// 40001 (serialization failure) only when r.engine is \"cockroachdb\" — on\n// \"postgres\" it runs fn exactly once. See retry.go.\nfunc (r *repository) withRetry(ctx context.Context, fn func(*sql.Tx) error) error {\n\treturn withRetry(ctx, r.db, r.engine, fn)\n}\n"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-36/internal/dao/postgres/retry.go",
  "content": "package postgres\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"database/sql\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\n\t\"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgconn\"\n)\n\n// sqlstateSerializationFailure is the SQLSTATE CockroachDB (and Postgres,\n// though Postgres at READ COMMITTED never produces it) returns when a\n// transaction must be retried, per multi-db-strategy.md §4.\nconst sqlstateSerializationFailure = \"40001\"\n\nconst maxRetries = 5\n\n// withRetry is the unexported helper inside this package that every write\n// method runs its statement inside (§4, \"Where the wrapper sits\"). It is\n// NOT in the composite and NOT above the interface boundary — it wraps a\n// single transaction attempt and retries only when engine is\n// \"cockroachdb\" and the failure is a genuine SQLSTATE 40001. On\n// \"postgres\" it calls fn exactly once. Search never calls this — it's\n// read-only and never encounters 40001 (§4).\nfunc withRetry(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB, e engine, fn func(*sql.Tx) error) error {\n\tattempts := 1\n\tif e == engineCockroach {\n\t\tattempts = maxRetries\n\t}\n\n\tvar lastErr error\n\tfor i := 0; i < attempts; i++ {\n\t\tlastErr = runOnce(ctx, db, fn)\n\t\tif lastErr == nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tif e != engineCockroach || !isRetryable(lastErr) {\n\t\t\treturn lastErr\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Retryable on CockroachDB: loop and try again. No backoff sleep\n\t\t// here — a serialization failure is not a load signal the way a\n\t\t// connection error is, and 05's contract does not ask for one;\n\t\t// keeping the retry bounded (maxRetries) is what prevents an\n\t\t// unbounded loop.\n\t}\n\treturn lastErr\n}\n\nfunc runOnce(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB, fn func(*sql.Tx) error) error {\n\ttx, err := db.BeginTx(ctx, nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tif err := fn(tx); err != nil {\n\t\t_ = tx.Rollback()\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\treturn tx.Commit()\n}\n\n// isRetryable reports whether err is a genuine SQLSTATE 40001, not a\n// string match on any error message.\nfunc isRetryable(err error) bool {\n\tvar pgErr *pgconn.PgError\n\tif errors.As(err, &pgErr) {\n\t\treturn pgErr.Code == sqlstateSerializationFailure\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-36/internal/dao/postgres/errors.go",
  "content": "package postgres\n\nimport (\n\t\"database/sql\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\n\t\"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgconn\"\n\n\t\"loginid-takehome/internal/dao\"\n)\n\n// Constraint names, pinned because they are our own DDL artifact, not the\n// engine's (multi-db-strategy.md §4). Named identically in this package's\n// schema and referenced here so translation never depends on table/column\n// position, which can drift independently between backends.\nconst (\n\tconstraintUsernameUnique = \"uq_user_credential_username\"\n\tconstraintMethodFK       = \"fk_user_credential_method\"\n\tconstraintSecretState    = \"ck_user_credential_secret_state\"\n)\n\nconst (\n\tsqlstateUniqueViolation     = \"23505\"\n\tsqlstateForeignKeyViolation = \"23503\"\n\tsqlstateCheckViolation      = \"23514\"\n)\n\n// translateError maps a driver error to one of the seven dao sentinels by\n// structured SQLSTATE code plus our own constraint name — never by\n// matching free-text error messages (multi-db-strategy.md §4). Anything\n// that isn't a recognized driver error is wrapped with %w and passed\n// through. The raw driver error (including any Postgres DETAIL/Key(...)=\n// (...) text) is deliberately never included in the sentinel path — only\n// the structured code and constraint name are inspected, and neither is\n// echoed into the returned sentinel, so a caller logging the sentinel\n// never logs PII (decisions/error-semantics.md F50).\nfunc translateError(err error) error {\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tif errors.Is(err, sql.ErrNoRows) {\n\t\treturn dao.ErrNotFound\n\t}\n\n\tvar pgErr *pgconn.PgError\n\tif errors.As(err, &pgErr) {\n\t\tswitch pgErr.Code {\n\t\tcase sqlstateUniqueViolation:\n\t\t\tif pgErr.ConstraintName == constraintUsernameUnique {\n\t\t\t\treturn dao.ErrDuplicateUsername\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// Any other unique violation (a primary-key collision) is the\n\t\t\t// internal-error-only sentinel — never caller-reachable, since\n\t\t\t// no caller supplies an ID (05's ruling on Oren's finding #1).\n\t\t\treturn dao.ErrAlreadyExists\n\t\tcase sqlstateForeignKeyViolation:\n\t\t\tif pgErr.ConstraintName == constraintMethodFK {\n\t\t\t\treturn dao.ErrInvalidMethod\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn dao.ErrInvalidArgument\n\t\tcase sqlstateCheckViolation:\n\t\t\tif pgErr.ConstraintName == constraintSecretState {\n\t\t\t\treturn dao.ErrInvalidCredential\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn dao.ErrInvalidArgument\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Not a recognized structured condition — wrap for logs, sanitized:\n\t// only the SQLSTATE code (if any) travels with it, never the raw\n\t// message/DETAIL text.\n\tif pgErr != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"postgres: driver error (sqlstate %s): %w\", pgErr.Code, errUnclassified)\n\t}\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"postgres: %w\", errUnclassified)\n}\n\nvar errUnclassified = errors.New(\"unclassified driver error\")\n"
}

Call only after ValidateProfileQuery passes.\nfunc EffectiveLimit(q ProfileQuery) int {\n\tif q.Limit == 0 {\n\t\treturn 50\n\t}\n\treturn q.Limit\n}\n"

{
  "command": "grep -n -i \"orphan\" /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-36/05-data-ops/pii-governance.md | head -20",
  "description": "Find orphan definition in pii-governance.md"
}
result
34:| `user_profile`, `source='idp_cache'`, **no referencing `user_credential`** (orphaned cache) | **`created_at`**, deliberately not `updated_at` | **7 days** since creation | Engineering: lookup residue, not an account — someone called `/identity`, we cached the answer, nothing was ever built on it. **The clock is `created_at` because `updated_at` is touched by the read and re-hydration path: an orphan re-read on any schedule would keep resetting its own expiry and never die.** Closing that loop is the entire reason this row exists | Hard delete | Nothing — by definition it has no children | unknown | Proposed |
54:Re-hydration moves `updated_at`, so an actively-used cache row can live indefinitely. That is correct, and it must be written down, because it means the 30-day window measures **time since last use**. It is also exactly why the orphan row is keyed on `created_at`: the two rules are one rule seen from two sides — a row that is still being used should survive, and a row that is only being *re-read by the sweep's own neighbours* should not be able to refresh itself into immortality.
60:| **Bulk retention sweep** (scheduled) | `source` = the class, plus that class's clock older than that class's window; orphan class additionally requires no referencing credential. **One class per pass — never a mixed-class sweep**, because a single predicate spanning two clocks is how the wrong window gets applied to the wrong rows | Many rows | Cascade delete, implicit and unmentioned in the predicate — the sweep selects profiles and destroys credentials as a side effect. Stated because it is invisible at the point of use | **Hard delete** | One row in a separate `deletion_log`: profile UUID, `source`, `deleted_at`, reason `retention_sweep`, job run id. **No name, phone or address** | Schedule it; run it per class; emit the metrics below | **Nothing errors.** No exception, no alert, no symptom — rows accumulate and the policy is fiction nobody can see is fiction |

\"idp_cache_orphan\" (an idp_cache row nothing\n// references) keys on created_at specifically so re-reads can't refresh\n// its own expiry.\nfunc clockColumnFor(class dao.RetentionClass) (column string, extraWhere string, ok bool) {\n\tswitch class {\n\tcase dao.RetentionDirect:\n\t\treturn \"updated_at\", \"source = 'direct'\", true\n\tcase dao.RetentionIDPCache:\n\t\treturn \"updated_at\", \"source = 'idp_cache' AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM user_credential WHERE user_credential.user_id = user_profile.id)\", true\n\tcase dao.RetentionIDPCacheOrphan:\n\t\treturn \"created_at\", \"source = 'idp_cache' AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM user_credential WHERE user_credential.user_id = user_profile.id)\", true\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn \"\", \"\", false\n\t}\n}\n\n// DeleteExpired batches one retention class per call (§3c). Bounded by\n// maxRows, one transaction per batch — never one per sweep. Never call\n// with a request-scoped context (enforced by the caller, per §3c).\nfunc (r *repository) DeleteExpired(ctx context.Context, class dao.RetentionClass, olderThan time.Time, maxRows int) (dao.SweepResult, error) {\n\tif maxRows < 1 || maxRows > 10_000 {\n\t\treturn dao.SweepResult{}, dao.ErrInvalidArgument\n\t}\n\tclockColumn, extraWhere, ok := clockColumnFor(class)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn dao.SweepResult{}, dao.ErrInvalidArgument\n\t}\n\n\tvar result dao.SweepResult\n\terr := r.withRetry(ctx, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {\n\t\trows, err := tx.QueryContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t\"SELECT id FROM user_profile WHERE \"+extraWhere+\" AND \"+clockColumn+\" < $1 LIMIT $2\",\n\t\t\tolderThan, maxRows)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn translateError(err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tvar ids []string\n\t\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\t\tvar id string\n\t\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&id); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\trows.Close()\n\t\t\t\treturn translateError(err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tids = append(ids, id)\n\t\t}\n\t\trows.Close()\n\t\tif err := rows.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn translateError(err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\texamined := len(ids)\n\t\tdeleted := 0\n\t\tfor _, id := range ids {\n\t\t\tres, err := tx.ExecContext(ctx, \"DELETE FROM user_profile WHERE id = $1\", id)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn translateError(err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tn, err := res.RowsAffected()\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn translateError(err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif n == 0 {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tdeleted++\n\t\t\tif _, err := tx.ExecContext(ctx, `\n\t\t\t\tINSERT INTO deletion_log (profile_id, source, reason, external_ref, job_run_id, deleted_at)\n\t\t\t\tVALUES ($1, $2, 'retention_sweep', NULL, NULL, now())`,\n\t\t\t\tid, string(class)); Sep 12, 7:24 PM PDTRenata Cole
Bug: my unit tests pass `db=nil`, but `withRetry` calls `db.BeginTx` internally, which would panic. Refactoring to separate the pure retry-loop logic from transaction management, so it's actually testable without a DB.
Write /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-36/internal/dao/postgres/retry.go
input
{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-36/internal/dao/postgres/retry.go",
  "content": "package postgres\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"database/sql\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\n\t\"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgconn\"\n)\n\n// sqlstateSerializationFailure is the SQLSTATE CockroachDB (and Postgres,\n// though Postgres at READ COMMITTED never produces it) returns when a\n// transaction must be retried, per multi-db-strategy.md §4.\nconst sqlstateSerializationFailure = \"40001\"\n\nconst maxRetries = 5\n\n// withRetry is the unexported helper inside this package that every write\n// method runs its statement inside (§4, \"Where the wrapper sits\"). It is\n// NOT in the composite and NOT above the interface boundary — it wraps a\n// single transaction attempt and retries only when engine is\n// \"cockroachdb\" and the failure is a genuine SQLSTATE 40001. On\n// \"postgres\" it calls fn exactly once. Search never calls this — it's\n// read-only and never encounters 40001 (§4).\nfunc withRetry(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB, e engine, fn func(*sql.Tx) error) error {\n\treturn retryLoop(e, func() error {\n\t\treturn runOnce(ctx, db, fn)\n\t})\n}\n\n// retryLoop is the pure engine-conditional retry policy, separated from\n// transaction management so it's unit-testable without a live *sql.DB.\nfunc retryLoop(e engine, attempt func() error) error {\n\tattempts := 1\n\tif e == engineCockroach {\n\t\tattempts = maxRetries\n\t}\n\n\tvar lastErr error\n\tfor i := 0; i < attempts; i++ {\n\t\tlastErr = attempt()\n\t\tif lastErr == nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tif e != engineCockroach || !isRetryable(lastErr) {\n\t\t\treturn lastErr\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Retryable on CockroachDB: loop and try again. No backoff sleep\n\t\t// here — a serialization failure is not a load signal the way a\n\t\t// connection error is, and 05's contract does not ask for one;\n\t\t// keeping the retry bounded (maxRetries) is what prevents an\n\t\t// unbounded loop.\n\t}\n\treturn lastErr\n}\n\nfunc runOnce(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB, fn func(*sql.Tx) error) error {\n\ttx, err := db.BeginTx(ctx, nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tif err := fn(tx); err != nil {\n\t\t_ = tx.Rollback()\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\treturn tx.Commit()\n}\n\n// isRetryable reports whether err is a genuine SQLSTATE 40001, not a\n// string match on any error message.\nfunc isRetryable(err error) bool {\n\tvar pgErr *pgconn.PgError\n\tif errors.As(err, &pgErr) {\n\t\treturn pgErr.Code == sqlstateSerializationFailure\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n"
}

1	package postgres
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"database/sql"
6		"errors"
7		"testing"
8	
9		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgconn"
10	)

12	// fakeRetryableErr constructs a *pgconn.PgError with the given SQLSTATE,
13	// wrapped the way a real driver error would be, without needing a live DB.
14	func fakeErr(code string) error {
15		return &pgconn.PgError{Code: code}
16	}
17	
18	func TestWithRetry_PostgresCallsOnce(t *testing.T) {
19		calls := 0
20		err := retryLoop(enginePostgres, func() error {
21			calls++
22			return fakeErr(sqlstateSerializationFailure)
23		})
24		if calls != 1 {
25			t.Errorf("calls = %d, want 1 — postgres engine must not retry", calls)
26		}
27		if !errors.As(err, new(*pgconn.PgError)) {
28			t.Errorf("expected the underlying error returned, got %v", err)
29		}
30	}
31	
32	func TestWithRetry_CockroachRetriesOnSerializationFailure(t *testing.T) {
33		calls := 0
34		err := withRetry(context.Background(), nil, engineCockroach, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {
35			calls++
36			return fakeErr(sqlstateSerializationFailure)
37		})
38		if calls != maxRetries {
39			t.Errorf("calls = %d, want %d — cockroachdb engine must retry a genuine 40001 up to the bound", calls, maxRetries)
40		}
41		if err == nil {
42			t.Error("expected an error after exhausting retries, got nil")
43		}
44	}
45	
46	func TestWithRetry_CockroachDoesNotRetryNonRetryableError(t *testing.T) {
47		calls := 0
48		err := withRetry(context.Background(), nil, engineCockroach, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {
49			calls++
50			return fakeErr(sqlstateUniqueViolation)
51		})
52		if calls != 1 {
53			t.Errorf("calls = %d, want 1 — a non-40001 error must not trigger a retry even on cockroachdb", calls)
54		}
55		if err == nil {
56			t.Error("expected an error, got nil")
57		}
58	}
59	
60	func TestWithRetry_SucceedsWithoutRetry(t *testing.T) {
61		calls := 0
62		err := withRetry(context.Background(), nil, engineCockroach, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {
63			calls++
64			return nil
65		})
66		if err != nil {
67			t.Errorf("unexpected error: %v", err)
68		}
69		if calls != 1 {
70			t.Errorf("calls = %d, want 1", calls)
71		}
72	}
73	
74	func TestIsRetryable(t *testing.T) {
75		if !isRetryable(fakeErr(sqlstateSerializationFailure)) {
76			t.Error("40001 should be retryable")
77		}
78		if isRetryable(fakeErr(sqlstateUniqueViolation)) {
79			t.Error("23505 should not be retryable")
80		}
81		if isRetryable(errors.New("some other error")) {
82			t.Error("a non-pgconn error should not be retryable")
83		}
84	}

1	# DDL review checklist — what 05 checks before a migration PR merges
2	
3	Owner: 05-data-ops (Priya Nandakumar). Audience: **track 03, before writing migrations** — not only 05 at review time. Published ahead of LT-36/LT-37 so the checks are known in advance rather than discovered in review; the same reasoning as amendment A1, where flagging a contract ambiguity before implementation cost one message and finding it at review would have cost a rewrite.
4	
5	**This introduces no new decisions.** Every item derives from `./multi-db-strategy.md` (the contract), `./migration-approach.md`, or `./pii-governance.md`, and each line names its source. If an item here disagrees with the contract, the contract wins and I have made an error — tell me and I will amend.
6	
7	## A. Layout
8	
9	1. **`shared/` contains seed data only — no `CREATE TABLE`.** All DDL lives in `postgres/` and `sqlite/`. (`migration-approach.md` §2, corrected under consistency-pass finding F28.)
10	2. **`postgres/` serves PostgreSQL *and* CockroachDB.** No third directory. (Contract §9 of the out-of-scope list.)
11	3. **Two goose invocations, separate version tables** (`goose_db_version_shared`, `goose_db_version_<driver>`). One shared table across two directories interleaves independent sequences and corrupts history. (`migration-approach.md` §2.)
12	
13	## B. Every constraint is named — this one is load-bearing
14	
15	4. **No anonymous constraints anywhere.** Error translation matches on SQLSTATE *plus the constraint name*, never on message text, because CockroachDB's message and `DETAIL` structure are not portable and its SQLSTATEs have a documented history of drift. An unnamed constraint is a sentinel that cannot be produced. (Contract §4; `research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md` §3.)
16	5. Names the contract already fixes: `uq_user_credential_username`, `fk_user_credential_method`, `ck_user_credential_secret_state`, `ck_user_profile_phone_e164`, `ck_deletion_log_reason`, and `ck_user_profile_{street_address,locality,region,postal_code}_nonempty`. (Contract §5.)
17	
18	## C. The three differences that already bit us in design
19	
20	These are the defects the behavioural conformance suite exists to catch. Each was invisible when either implementation was read alone.
21	
22	6. **Collation is explicit on `user_profile.name` in both engines** — `COLLATE "C"` on Postgres/CockroachDB, `COLLATE BINARY` on SQLite — in the DDL, not only in `ORDER BY`. Without it, page 1 of a search returns different rows per backend: Postgres sorts by database collation, SQLite's default byte order puts every uppercase letter before every lowercase one. (Contract §6.3.)
23	7. **The trigram index is an expression index on `LOWER(name)` with `gin_trgm_ops`, not on the bare column** — a GIN index on bare `name` is not used by a `LOWER(name)` predicate, so the accelerator would accelerate nothing. Postgres/CockroachDB only; **absent on SQLite, omitted rather than faked.** (Contract §5 Indexes, §6.3.)
24	8. **`country` carries `CHECK (country IS NULL OR (country = upper(country) AND length(country) = 2))`** — normalization at write, not only at query. A stored `us` is silently invisible to a search for `US`. SQLite's `upper()` is ASCII-only, which is sufficient for alpha-2 and is written down so nobody assumes it generalizes. (Contract §6.5.)
25	
26	## D. Per-column checks
27	
28	9. **UUID primary keys on every backend** — server-generated on Postgres/CockroachDB, app-generated in Go before insert on SQLite. No per-backend PK strategy. (Contract §6.6.)
29	10. **Absence is single-valued: NULL means absent, `''` is invalid.** All four address columns nullable with non-empty `CHECK`s; `name` `NOT NULL` with `length(trim(name)) > 0`. (Contract §5.)
30	11. **`phone`** — regex `CHECK` on Postgres/CockroachDB, the `GLOB` form given in contract §5 on SQLite. Both named `ck_user_profile_phone_e164`. Exact-match only per finding F9; the B-tree index would support prefix, the contract does not offer it.
31	12. **`secret_state`** — the table-level `CHECK` tying `secret_state` to `secret`/`hash_algo`/`hash_cost` must be present, or a row with a secret and no algorithm becomes representable. (Contract §5.)
32	13. **Timestamps** — `TIMESTAMPTZ` on Postgres/CockroachDB, `TEXT` RFC3339 UTC on SQLite.
33	14. **Booleans** — native on Postgres/CockroachDB, `INTEGER` 0/1 on SQLite, mapped at the DAO boundary.
34	
35	## E. Foreign keys and `deletion_log`
36	
37	15. **`user_credential.user_id` → `user_profile(id)` `ON DELETE CASCADE`**; `method_id` → `auth_method(id)` **RESTRICT**. The asymmetry is deliberate and documented: deleting a profile destroys its credentials, deleting a credential leaves the profile standing. (Contract §6.2.)
38	16. **`deletion_log.profile_id` has NO foreign key** — the row it names is meant not to exist. (Contract §5.)
39	17. **`deletion_log` carries no name, phone, address, username or secret column.** A conformance test asserts this; the DDL must not make it false. (Contract §6.11.)
40	
41	## F. Seed data
42	
43	18. **`auth_method` seeded with one `'password'` row, idempotent via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (name) DO NOTHING`.** In `shared/`, as a migration — not a runtime bootstrap. (`migration-approach.md` §4.)
44	19. **New auth methods are inserted as data, never added as migrations.** A migration to add `passkey` means something upstream has been misunderstood — flag it. (Contract §6.7.)
45	
46	## What I will not do at review
47	
48	Re-open decisions. If the DDL is correct against this list and something still looks wrong to me, that is a **contract amendment** with a signed entry in the amendments log, not a review comment asking for a change the contract does not require. The contract is authoritative and the code follows it; where the code has found the contract wrong — which has happened three times, each time caught by the person implementing against it rather than by anyone reading it — the fix is to amend the document.
49	
50	---
51	
52	*Work-By: Priya Nandakumar (05 Data Ops lead, Claude Opus 5). Derived from existing rulings; no new decisions. Items 6–8 trace to defects found by Yusuf Karadag (DDL/nullability review) and the S2 adversarial pair with Anders Vogel; item 17 to Beatriz Achterberg's retention work; the F28 layout correction to the cross-track consistency pass.*

Bash grep -n -i "F28\|shared/\|postgres/\|sqlite/\|migrations/" /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-work
input
{
  "command": "grep -n -i \"F28\\|shared/\\|postgres/\\|sqlite/\\|migrations/\" /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-36/05-data-ops/migration-approach.md | head -30",
