}
result
18:migrations/
19:  shared/     # seed data only — no CREATE TABLE
20:  postgres/   # all DDL for Postgres + CockroachDB, plus the trigram index
21:  sqlite/     # all DDL for SQLite
24:**Corrected after the cross-track consistency pass (F28).** This section previously put every `CREATE TABLE` in `shared/`, with `postgres/` holding one index file. That was wrong, and wrong in a way the rest of this track's own work had already ruled out: the field-level schema in `multi-db-strategy.md` §5 gives **every** table per-engine DDL — `UUID DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()` vs `TEXT`, `TEXT COLLATE "C"` vs `TEXT COLLATE BINARY`, a regex `CHECK` vs a `GLOB` `CHECK`, `TIMESTAMPTZ` vs `TEXT`, `BYTEA` vs `BLOB`, `BOOLEAN` vs `INTEGER`. A single shared `CREATE TABLE` series cannot express that. SQLite's type affinity would quietly swallow the differing *type names*, which is what made the original claim look survivable, but it does nothing for collation clauses or CHECK syntax — those are hard syntax differences, not affinity.
26:The document already carried the right answer as a hypothetical fallback ("per-backend `CREATE TABLE` files and `shared/` reduced to seed data") and left it contingent on an open item marked "under review in story S1". **S1 closed, the review resolved it in favour of the fallback, and this document was not updated** — so a document marked "final" was carrying a provisional default that its own dependency had already overruled. That is the failure, and it is worse than the layout error: the fallback was correct, written down, and left unpromoted.
30:- **`shared/`** carries the `auth_method` seed row and nothing else. No `CREATE TABLE`.
31:- **`postgres/`** carries the full DDL for `auth_method`, `user_profile`, `user_credential` and `deletion_log` in Postgres/CockroachDB types, plus the `pg_trgm` trigram expression index on `LOWER(name)`.
32:- **`sqlite/`** carries the same four tables in SQLite types and collations, and no trigram index — there is no SQLite equivalent and it is omitted rather than faked.
34:**Nothing in §3–§6 changes, and neither does 04's design**, exactly as the earlier text promised: the two-invocation mechanism, the separate version tables, R1–R5 and the seed-data rule are untouched. R3's worked example still holds and in fact reads better now — a service can have `shared/` current with the `pg_trgm` index unapplied, which is why fail-closed must check both version tables. (`pg_trgm` is a PostgreSQL extension that indexes three-character fragments of text so that substring searches like `LIKE '%smith%'` can use an index instead of scanning every row — it is what makes name search fast. CockroachDB has the same capability built in; SQLite has no equivalent, so the index is omitted there rather than faked.). That parity gap is documented and deliberate (`./multi-db-strategy.md`); SQLite is the local/dev/demo backend, not a production peer.
36:**Mechanism 04 needs:** goose applies one directory per invocation, so a migration run is **two invocations** — `shared/` then the backend directory — and each needs its own version table (`goose -table goose_db_version_shared`, then `-table goose_db_version_<driver>`). One shared table across two directories would interleave version numbers from independent sequences and corrupt the history. Both invocations must succeed for the run to count as successful.
38:**That open item is now closed.** It asked whether a single `shared/` DDL series survives all three backends, given that SQLite does not enforce column types the way PostgreSQL does — it assigns each column a loose "type affinity" and falls back to NUMERIC for any type name it does not recognise, `UUID` included. The answer turned out to be no, and for a reason narrower than type affinity: affinity would have absorbed the type names, but collation clauses and CHECK syntax differ outright. Resolved in favour of the per-backend split above. No open items remain in this document.
52:**R3. The service fails closed when the schema is behind — checking BOTH version tables.** §2 creates two independent version sequences (`shared/` and the backend directory), so "the applied version" is two numbers, not one. The check must compare both, and must know which backend directory applies to the engine it is connected to. Checking only `shared/` would let a service pass with the `pg_trgm` index unapplied — caught by an objection turn, and it would have been a passing check that proved nothing. On startup the service compares both applied versions against the versions it was built against and refuses to serve if either is behind, rather than serving traffic against a schema missing a column it will query. The requirement is mine; whether the expected version is embedded at build time or derived from the migration files shipped in the image is 03's and 04's call.
64:The `auth_method` table is seeded with one row, `"password"`. It ships as a migration file in `shared/`, not as a runtime bootstrap in the service — a service that writes its own lookup rows on boot is a service that can write them wrongly, concurrently, on every replica.

Applying the actual migration files\n// in tests is what keeps this test harness honest: if the migrations\n// drift from what the Go code expects, the integration tests fail, not\n// just a hand-maintained fixture.\nfunc migrationSQL(t *testing.T, path string) string {\n\tt.Helper()\n\tb, err := os.ReadFile(path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"reading migration %s: %v\", path, err)\n\t}\n\tcontent := string(b)\n\tupStart := strings.Index(content, \"-- +goose Up\")\n\tdownStart := strings.Index(content, \"-- +goose Down\")\n\tif upStart == -1 || downStart == -1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"migration %s missing +goose Up/Down markers\", path)\n\t}\n\treturn content[upStart+len(\"-- +goose Up\") : downStart]\n}\n\n// setupDB connects to TEST_POSTGRES_DSN (skipping the test if unset — real\n// Postgres/CockroachDB verification is this package's own responsibility;\n// LT-38's cross-backend suite proves behavior matches SQLite, not that\n// this package can reach a database at all), applies the real migration\n// files into a fresh, uniquely-named schema, and tears the schema down on\n// test cleanup so tests never collide with each other.\nfunc setupDB(t *testing.T) *sql.DB {\n\tt.Helper()\n\tdsn := os.Getenv(\"TEST_POSTGRES_DSN\")\n\tif dsn == \"\" {\n\t\tt.Skip(\"TEST_POSTGRES_DSN not set — skipping Postgres integration test (see refinement/LT-36-LT-37.md: real-backend verification is this package's responsibility, run manually or in CI once wired)\")\n\t}\n\n\tdb, err := sql.Open(\"pgx\", dsn)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"opening TEST_POSTGRES_DSN: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tt.Cleanup(func() { db.Close() })\n\n\tschemaName := fmt.Sprintf(\"test_%d\", rand.Int63())\n\tif _, err := db.Exec(\"CREATE SCHEMA \" + schemaName); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"setting search_path: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\trepoRoot := repoRootFromThisFile(t)\n\tpostgresMigration := migrationSQL(t, filepath.Join(repoRoot, \"migrations\", \"postgres\", \"00001_initial_schema.sql\"))\n\tif _, err := db.Exec(postgresMigration); A schema-visibility issue with `pg_trgm`, not a logic bug — the extension installs once per database, and subsequent test schemas can't see its operator class without `public` on their search path. Fixing the test harness.
Edit /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-36/internal/dao/postgres/testutil_test.go

  "new_string": "\t// Include \"public\" on the path: pg_trgm's CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT\n\t// EXISTS in the migration is a no-op after the first test schema\n\t// creates it, and an extension's objects (gin_trgm_ops) live in\n\t// whichever schema was current at creation time — later test schemas\n\t// need \"public\" on their path to see it, not just their own name.\n\tif _, err := db.Exec(\"SET search_path TO \" + schemaName + \", public\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"setting search_path: %v\", err)\n\t}"

package sqlite // import "modernc.org/sqlite"

type Error struct {
	// Has unexported fields.
}
    Error represents sqlite library error code.

func (e *Error) Code() int
func (e *Error) Error() string
---
package sqlite // import "modernc.org/sqlite"

Package sqlite is a sql/database driver using a CGo-free port of the C SQLite3
library.

SQLite is an in-process implementation of a self-contained, serverless,
zero-configuration, transactional SQL database engine.

# Pluggable page cache

The package exposes a Go-facing wrapper for SQLite's SQLITE_CONFIG_PCACHE2
mechanism. Applications can supply their own page cache implementation by
registering a PageCache before the first sql.Open via RegisterPageCache. See
the docstrings on PageCache, Cache, and Page for the contract; the binding owns
the sqlite3_pcache_page stub on behalf of the implementation and re-consults
Cache.Fetch on every SQLite request, so a bounded and evicting purgeable cache
works as the C contract intends.

# OFD locking (Linux)

On Linux the library can take Open File Description (OFD) locks instead of POSIX
record locks on database files, which stops an unrelated os.File close anywhere
in the process from silently stripping SQLite's transaction locks. The switch
is process-wide, off by default, and must happen before the first connection
is opened: set MODERNC_SQLITE_OFD_LOCK=1 in the environment the process starts
with, or call OFDLocking from Go. See the OFDLocking documentation for the full
contract.

# Fragile modernc.org/libc dependency

When you import this package you should use in your go.mod file the exact same
version of modernc.org/libc as seen in the go.mod file of this repository.

See the discussion at https://gitlab.com/cznic/sqlite/-/issues/177 for more
details.

# Thanks

This project is sponsored by Schleibinger Geräte Teubert u. Greim GmbH by
allowing one of the maintainers to work on it also in office hours.

NOT NULL,\n\tcreated_at TEXT NOT NULL\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE user_profile (\n\tid TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n\tname TEXT COLLATE BINARY NOT NULL CHECK (length(trim(name)) > 0),\n\tphone TEXT CONSTRAINT ck_user_profile_phone_e164 CHECK (phone IS NULL OR (phone GLOB '+[1-9]*' AND length(phone) BETWEEN 8 AND 16 AND NOT phone GLOB '*[^+0-9]*')),\n\tstreet_address TEXT CONSTRAINT ck_user_profile_street_address_nonempty CHECK (street_address IS NULL OR length(trim(street_address)) > 0),\n\tlocality TEXT CONSTRAINT ck_user_profile_locality_nonempty CHECK (locality IS NULL OR ck_user_profile_postal_code_nonempty CHECK (postal_code IS NULL OR length(trim(postal_code)) > 0),\n\tcountry TEXT CHECK (country IS NULL OR (country = upper(country) AND length(country) = 2)),\n\tsource TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (source IN ('direct','idp_cache')),\n\tcreated_at TEXT NOT NULL,\n\tupdated_at TEXT NOT NULL\n);\n\nCREATE INDEX idx_user_profile_phone ON user_profile (phone);\n-- No trigram/expression index here — SQLite has no GIN equivalent;\n-- Search falls back to a full scan, documented in the contract.\n\nCREATE TABLE user_credential (\n\tid TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n\tuser_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES user_profile(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n\tusername TEXT NOT NULL,\n\tmethod_id TEXT NOT NULL CONSTRAINT fk_user_credential_method REFERENCES auth_method(id),\n\tsecret BLOB,\n\tsecret_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (secret_state IN ('none','set','revoked')),\n\thash_algo TEXT,\n\thash_cost INTEGER,\n\tcreated_at TEXT NOT NULL,\n\tupdated_at TEXT NOT NULL,\n\tCONSTRAINT ck_user_credential_secret_state CHECK (\n\t\t(secret_state = 'set'  AND secret IS NOT NULL AND hash_algo IS NOT NULL AND hash_cost IS NOT NULL)\n\t\tOR\n\t\t(secret_state IN ('none','revoked') AND secret IS NULL AND hash_algo IS NULL AND hash_cost IS NULL)\n\t)\n);\n\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_user_credential_username ON user_credential (LOWER(username));\nCREATE INDEX idx_user_credential_user_id ON user_credential (user_id);\nCREATE INDEX idx_user_credential_method_id ON user_credential (method_id);\n\nCREATE TABLE deletion_log (\n\tid TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n\tprofile_id TEXT NOT NULL,\n\tsource TEXT NOT NULL,\n\treason TEXT NOT NULL CONSTRAINT ck_deletion_log_reason CHECK (reason IN ('retention_sweep','subject_request')),\n\texternal_ref TEXT,\n\tjob_run_id TEXT,\n\tdeleted_at TEXT NOT NULL\n);\n-- No name, phone, address, username or secret column — contract §6.11.\n\n-- +goose Down\nDROP TABLE deletion_log;\nDROP TABLE user_credential;\nDROP TABLE user_profile;\nDROP TABLE   "content": "package dao\n\nimport \"strings\"\n\n// ValidateProfileQuery enforces the ErrInvalidQuery triggers enumerated in\n// multi-db-strategy.md §4: a non-nil filter that is empty after trimming;\n// Limit < 0 or > 100; Offset < 0 or > 10_000; Country not exactly two\n// characters. An all-nil query is legal. Both backends call this before\n// building any SQL, so the rule is identical across engines rather than\n// each package re-deriving it.\nfunc ValidateProfileQuery(q ProfileQuery) error {\n\tfor _, f := range []*string{q.Name, q.Phone, q.Region, q.Country} {\n\t\tif f != nil && strings.TrimSpace(*f) == \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn ErrInvalidQuery\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif q.Limit < 0 || q.Limit > 100 {\n\t\treturn ErrInvalidQuery\n\t}\n\tif q.Offset < 0 || q.Offset > 10_000 {\n\t\treturn ErrInvalidQuery\n\t}\n\tif q.Country != nil && len(*q.Country) != 2 {\n\t\treturn ErrInvalidQuery\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// EffectiveLimit returns 50 when Limit is 0 (deliberately, per §3a note 2),\n// otherwise Limit unchanged. Call only after ValidateProfileQuery passes.\nfunc EffectiveLimit(q ProfileQuery) int {\n\tif q.Limit == 0 {\n\t\treturn 50\n\t}\n\treturn q.Limit\n}\n"
}

A separate package from\n// postgres — SQLite diverges enough (no native UUID/BOOLEAN, no trigram\n// index, app-side ID generation) that forcing it through the Postgres\n// implementation would mean littering that code with backend\n// conditionals, which is the exact failure mode a repository-per-backend\n// split is meant to avoid.\npackage sqlite\n\nimport (\n\t\"database/sql\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\n\t_ \"modernc.org/sqlite\" // registers the \"sqlite\" database/sql driver\n\n\t\"loginid-takehome/internal/dao\"\n)\n\ntype repository struct {\n\tdb *sql.DB\n}\n\n// New opens a connection against dsn (a file path, or \":memory:\").\n// Registered with internal/dao's factory in register.go. There is no\n// retry wrapper here at all — not a no-op shim. SQLite has no\n// retryable-serialization error class, so a no-op would be ceremony\n// implying a shared abstraction that does not exist (contract §4).\nfunc New(dsn string) (dao.Repository, error) {\n\tdb, err := sql.Open(\"sqlite\", dsn)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"sqlite: opening connection: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tif _, err := db.Exec(\"PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON\"); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"sqlite: enabling foreign_keys: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\t// SQLite allows only one writer at a time; a single connection avoids\n\t// SQLITE_BUSY from this package's own concurrent use within a process.\n\tdb.SetMaxOpenConns(1)\n\treturn &repository{db: db}, nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *repository) Profiles() dao.ProfileRepository      { return profileRepo{r} }\nfunc (r *repository) Credentials() dao.CredentialRepository { return credentialRepo{r} }\nfunc (r *repository) Methods() dao.AuthMethodRepository     { return authMethodRepo{r} }\n\nfunc (r *repository) Close() error { return r.db.Close() }\n"

  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-37/internal/dao/sqlite/errors.go",
  "content": "package sqlite\n\nimport (\n\t\"database/sql\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\tsqlitedriver \"modernc.org/sqlite\"\n\n\t\"loginid-takehome/internal/dao\"\n)\n\n// Extended result codes, per SQLite's documented result-code table\n// (https://www.sqlite.org/rescode.html). modernc.org/sqlite's Error.Code()\n// returns the extended code, not just the primary SQLITE_CONSTRAINT class.\nconst (\n\tsqliteConstraintUnique     = 2067 // SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE\n\tsqliteConstraintPrimaryKey = 1555 // SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_PRIMARYKEY\n\tsqliteConstraintForeignKey = 787  // SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY\n\tsqliteConstraintCheck      = 275  // SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_CHECK\n)\n\nconst (\n\tconstraintUsernameUnique = \"uq_user_credential_username\"\n\tconstraintMethodFK       = \"fk_user_credential_method\"\n\tconstraintSecretState    = \"ck_user_credential_secret_state\"\n)\n\n// checkConstraintName extracts the constraint name from a SQLite CHECK\n// violation message. SQLite's message for a named constraint (defined as\n// `CONSTRAINT name CHECK (...)` in our own DDL) is deterministically\n// \"CHECK constraint failed: <name>\" — this is not free-text message\n// matching in the sense the contract prohibits; it's extracting a\n// structured field SQLite's own error format guarantees to contain\n// exactly the constraint name we ourselves wrote in DDL, the same\n// artifact Postgres gives us through PgError.ConstraintName directly.\nfunc checkConstraintName(msg string) (string, bool) {\n\tconst prefix = \"constraint failed: \"\n\ti := strings.Index(strings.ToLower(msg), prefix)\n\tif i == -1 {\n\t\treturn \"\", false\n\t}\n\treturn strings.TrimSpace(msg[i+len(prefix):]), true\n}\n\n// translateError maps a driver error to one of the seven dao sentinels by\n// structured extended result code plus our own constraint name — never by\n// matching arbitrary free-text. Mirrors postgres/errors.go's rule exactly\n// so both backends produce the same sentinel for the same logical\n// condition (multi-db-strategy.md §4).\nfunc translateError(err error) error {\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tif errors.Is(err, sql.ErrNoRows) {\n\t\treturn dao.ErrNotFound\n\t}\n\n\tvar sqErr *sqlitedriver.Error\n\tif errors.As(err, &sqErr) {\n\t\tswitch sqErr.Code() {\n\t\tcase sqliteConstraintUnique:\n\t\t\tif name, ok := uniqueConstraintName(sqErr.Error()); ok && name == constraintUsernameUnique {\n\t\t\t\treturn dao.ErrDuplicateUsername\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn dao.ErrDuplicateUsername // this schema's only UNIQUE index besides the PK is the username one\n\t\tcase sqliteConstraintPrimaryKey:\n\t\t\treturn dao.ErrAlreadyExists\n\t\tcase sqliteConstraintForeignKey:\n\t\t\t// SQLite's FK violation message does not name the FK\n\t\t\t// constraint; this schema has exactly one FK we translate\n\t\t\t// specifically (method_id) and one we don't need to\n\t\t\t// (user_id, never violated by application code since the\n\t\t\t// DAO always creates the profile first in the same\n\t\t\t// transaction) — so any FK violation reaching here is the\n\t\t\t// method FK.\n\t\t\treturn dao.ErrInvalidMethod\n\t\tcase sqliteConstraintCheck:\n\t\t\tif name, ok := checkConstraintName(sqErr.Error()); ok && name == constraintSecretState {\n\t\t\t\treturn dao.ErrInvalidCredential\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn dao.ErrInvalidArgument\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif sqErr != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"sqlite: driver error (code %d): %w\", sqErr.Code(), errUnclassified)\n\t}\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"sqlite: %w\", errUnclassified)\n}\n\n// uniqueConstraintName mirrors checkConstraintName's reasoning for the\n// uq_user_credential_username index: SQLite reports a UNIQUE violation on\n// a named index as \"UNIQUE constraint failed: <index-or-column-list>\".\n// This schema has only that one non-PK UNIQUE index, so the disambiguation\n// this function attempts is belt-and-suspenders, not load-bearing.\nfunc uniqueConstraintName(msg string) (string, bool) {\n\tconst marker = \"unique constraint failed:\"\n\ti := strings.Index(strings.ToLower(msg), marker)\n\tif i == -1 {\n\t\treturn \"\", false\n\t}\n\treturn strings.TrimSpace(msg[i+len(marker):]), true\n}\n\nvar errUnclassified = errors.New(\"unclassified driver error\")\n"
}
